People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I don’t know if that answers it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think so. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like… nothing good is really waiting for me, and I just kind of want to keep my head down. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself in the mirror, do you see your body as larger than it really is?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I look and think I’m bigger than I really am. Sometimes I don’t like what I see, but I’m not sure it’s that. Maybe I just look kind of… off to myself? I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, maybe more than I want to admit. I’m not really eating right lately—some days it’s just coffee and maybe a piece of bread. My pants have been kind of hanging loose on me. I know that probably sounds bad… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you skip meals, is it mostly because you’re not hungry?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I guess I just don’t feel hungry. Or I ignore it. I don’t want it to sound like I’m doing it on purpose, but some days I end up just having coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I realize my pants are loose. That’s… probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been losing your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I just hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I notice my pants are kind of hanging loose on me. So… yes, I guess my appetite’s been pretty low.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I used to like walking by the lake, and I had these little model kits I’d work on. Now they mostly just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else or something. Is that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t want to say the wrong thing, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself or wanting to die. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m not doing it on purpose, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like… nothing good is really waiting for me, and I just want to keep my head down. It’s been like that pretty much day after day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even little things feel like a lot. I mean, I guess I don’t really like the idea of it, but… I’m not sure. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat even when other people say you look thin?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I guess sometimes I don’t really see what they’re seeing, but I wouldn’t want to say I feel “fat,” exactly. Maybe I just… don’t feel right in my body? I don’t know if that answers it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think so. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like… nothing good is really waiting for me, and I just kind of want to keep my head down. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: When you look at yourself in the mirror, do you see your body as larger than it really is?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like I look and think I’m bigger than I really am. Sometimes I don’t like what I see, but I’m not sure it’s that. Maybe I just look kind of… off to myself? I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, maybe more than I want to admit. I’m not really eating right lately—some days it’s just coffee and maybe a piece of bread. My pants have been kind of hanging loose on me. I know that probably sounds bad… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you skip meals, is it mostly because you’re not hungry?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yeah… I guess I just don’t feel hungry. Or I ignore it. I don’t want it to sound like I’m doing it on purpose, but some days I end up just having coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I realize my pants are loose. That’s… probably not great, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you been losing your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I just hardly eat right anymore. Some days it’s just coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I notice my pants are kind of hanging loose on me. So… yes, I guess my appetite’s been pretty low.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I used to like walking by the lake, and I had these little model kits I’d work on. Now they mostly just sit there. I look at them and it’s like… they belong to somebody else or something. Is that what you mean by losing interest?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of wanting to die or hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t want to say the wrong thing, but I haven’t been thinking about hurting myself or wanting to die. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean… I’m not trying to. It’s more like I just don’t feel like eating much. Some days I’ll have coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I notice my pants are looser. I’m not doing it on purpose, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like… nothing good is really waiting for me, and I just want to keep my head down. It’s been like that pretty much day after day.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but even little things feel like a lot. Like taking out the trash, or getting myself over here to the clinic… it feels like my arms and legs are full of wet sand or something. I still try, but it’s been really hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid eating because it makes you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really anxious, no… I don’t think that’s it. It’s more like I just don’t feel like eating right. Some days I only have coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I notice my pants are hanging loose. I know that probably doesn’t sound great… but I’m not avoiding food because I’m scared of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have much interest in food or eating these days?\n\nPatient: Not much, no… I mean, I know I should eat, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing it on purpose. I just… hardly feel interested in food lately. Some days it’s just coffee and maybe a piece of bread, and then I realize my pants are kind of hanging loose on me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to think of it that way, like “worthless” sounds kind of harsh. But… yeah, I do get stuck thinking I’m no good. Losing that job just keeps playing in my head, and now my relatives have to help me, and I feel like I’ve messed everything up. I keep replaying every mistake, like if I’d done one thing different maybe I wouldn’t have ruined it all. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or talking more slowly lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess they have. My family says I’m moving kind of slow, like I’m walking through mud or something. I didn’t really notice it at first, or maybe I didn’t want to. And sometimes they ask me a simple question and I just… sit there for a while before I answer. I’m not trying to be difficult. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but I do feel guilty a lot. Mostly about losing that job and then needing my relatives to help me. I keep going back over every mistake, like maybe I ruined everything and I’m just… no good. I know maybe that sounds dramatic, but that’s how it keeps coming up in my head. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
